❯ back in June, I built a brand new conda/2026-06-08 module from scratch on     
  ALCF Sophia for all users to share, using Claude Code to help optimize the    
  installations and bump versions to the latest ones at the time                
  @alcf_sophia/build_monolithic_conda_module.sh . At the time, I was thinking   
  ahead to building something similar on ALCF Sirius (the test and dev machine  
  for ALCF Polaris), so I had Claude port some of the changes to                
  @alcf_polaris/build_monolithic_conda_module.sh. Now, I am actually going to   
  attempt this build, and need to bump the packages again. Also incorporate     
  some of the fixes we noticed when patching the Sirius/Polaris modules in the  
  last few days. I know PyTorch released 2.14.0 recently-- what other packages  
  need upgrading?                                                               
  also, what libraries do I need to download to @/soft/compilers/cudatoolkit    
  and @/soft/libraries/ ?                                                       
                                                                                
  let's tentatively call this new module conda/2026-09-17

● The Polaris build script and two new modulefiles are updated for
  conda/2026-09-17. Nothing is committed.

  Big decision: CUDA 13.0.3, not 12.9.1

  Sirius's driver 580.65.06 is CUDA 13.0. Toolkits 13.0.3 through 13.3.1 are
  already in /soft/compilers/cudatoolkit/ (nothing to download there). I picked
  13.0.3 because a newer toolkit's NVRTC/ptxas emits PTX the 13.0 driver can't
  JIT (cupy, XLA autotune). Bonus: the PE 26.03 GTL links libcudart.so.13, so
  the new module needs no shim dir. Note the Polaris 2026-06-10*.lua modulefiles
  describe a build that never happened.

  Downloads you need under /soft/libraries/ (I did not run these)

  Lib: cuDNN
  Version: 9.26.0.51 cuda13 (2026-09-09)
  Source: https://developer.download.nvidia.com/compute/cudnn/redist/cudnn/linux
  -x86_64/cudnn-linux-x86_64-9.26.0.51_cuda13-archive.tar.xz
  Extract to: cudnn/cudnn-cuda13-linux-x64-v9.26.0.51 (rename)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Lib: NCCL
  Version: 2.30.7-1 (what torch 2.14 cu13 ships)
  Source: https://developer.download.nvidia.com/compute/redist/nccl/v2.30.7/nccl
  _2.30.7-1+cuda13.3_x86_64.txz
  Extract to: nccl/nccl_2.30.7-1+cuda13.3_x86_64
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Lib: cuSPARSELt
  Version: 0.9.1.1 cuda13
  Source:
  https://developer.download.nvidia.com/compute/cusparselt/redist/libcusparse_lt
  /linux-x86_64/libcusparse_lt-linux-x86_64-0.9.1.1_cuda13-archive.tar.xz
  Extract to: cusparselt/ (keep archive name)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Lib: TensorRT
  Version: 10.16.1.11 cuda-13.2
  Source: developer portal login:
  TensorRT-10.16.1.11.Linux.x86_64-gnu.cuda-13.2.tar.gz
  Extract to: trt/TensorRT-10.16.1.11.Linux.x86_64-gnu.cuda-13.2

  NCCL only ships a +cuda13.3 CUDA 13 tarball; it's the same binary the torch
  cu130 wheels use on 580 drivers, so it should be fine. TRT only has
  cuda-12.9/13.2 tars for 10.16.1 and nothing in the env actually consumes it.
  No local NVSHMEM needed (TF hermetic downloads its own). The script now exits
  early if any of these dirs is missing.

  Package bumps (verified against upstream on 2026-09-16)

  - torch 2.14.0, torchvision 0.29.0, triton 3.8.0 pinned, jax 0.11.1 with
    mpi4jax v0.9.1.post1 pinned (the two lessons from 09-28), re-asserted with
    --no-deps at the end plus an import check.
  - TF stays 2.21.0 (still latest); hermetic set reuses Sophia's proven
    13.0.2/9.16.0/2.29.2/3.4.5, clang 22.1.4.
  - flash-attn 2.8.3.post1, TE v2.19, vLLM v0.29.0, FlashInfer v0.6.18.post1,
    DeepSpeed v0.19.6, verl v0.9.0, onnx 1.22.0, onnxruntime-gpu 1.30.0 (PyPI
    wheel is now CUDA 13), cuda-bindings tracks CUDA_VERSION_FULL, PyG cu130
    wheels enabled with --no-deps.
  - transformers pinned 5.10.4: the only window verl v0.9.0 (<5.11) and vLLM
    v0.29.0 (>=5.10.4) both accept.

  Fixes folded in from the last few days

  - Header: PrgEnv-gnu + gcc-native/14, module unload darshan xalt, the five
    CRAY_ACCEL_* exports instead of craype-accel-nvidia80. The mid-script
    PrgEnv-gnu reload and module load cuda dance are gone.
  - ldd | grep gtl tripwires after mpi4py and h5py; mpi4jax now built with
    CC=cc.
  - torch 2.14 requires python -m build --wheel --no-isolation; setup.py 
    bdist_wheel is now a shim that just prints instructions. torchvision uses
    pip wheel . --no-build-isolation --no-deps.
  - TENSORRT_INCLUDE_DIR typo fixed; TF_PYTHON_VERSION derived from the
    Miniforge python with a warning if it isn't 3.13.
  - Modulefiles 2026-09-17.lua and the hidden -aws-nccl-1.9.1 variant:
    cray-hdf5-parallel/1.14.3.9, gcc-native/14, CUDA 13 paths,
    NVTE_CUDA_INCLUDE_DIR, no shim. The aws variant points at the dir that
    actually exists; the 2025-09 aws variants reference a nonexistent
    v1.9.1-libfabric-1.22.0, and that plugin has an unresolved libhwloc.so.0 and
    was built against libfabric 1.22 while PE 26.03 has 2.3.1, so retest before
    unhiding.

  Risks to watch in the build log

  1. vLLM v0.29.0 pins torch 2.13.0 upstream; we build it against 2.14 via
     use_existing_torch.py. First suspect if vLLM misbehaves.
  2. Miniforge 26.7.2's base python: if it's 3.14, TF 2.21 wheels won't build.
  3. The cmake<4 pin is kept but may no longer be needed for torch 2.14.

  Run it on a compute node, then the 2-node harness in alcf_polaris/tests/ with
  module load conda/2026-09-17.

● Added. pip install "globus-compute-endpoint==4.9.0" "parsl==2026.02.23" goes
  right after libensemble in alcf_polaris/build_monolithic_conda_module.sh, plus
  a pip check and a version print after the final conda list.

  Two things to know about that pair:

  - The endpoint hard-pins parsl==2026.2.23 (same release, PyPI-normalized),
    psutil<6 and pyzmq<=26.1.0. Installing both in one resolve avoids pip
    bouncing between them. Nothing later in the script pins psutil or pyzmq, so
    the caps should hold; the trailing pip check will show it if vLLM or verl
    moved them.
  - 4.9.0 is from 2026-03-25; the current endpoint is 4.17.0. I kept your
    requested pin and noted it in the comment as the service-validated version.

● TensorRT is effectively dead weight in this stack; the only live consumer is
  ONNX Runtime's TensorRT execution provider.

  What consumes it today (conda/2025-09-25)

  - onnxruntime-gpu 1.23.0 ships libonnxruntime_providers_tensorrt.so, which
    resolves libnvinfer.so.10 from the TRT 10.13.3.9 dir the modulefile puts on
    LD_LIBRARY_PATH. TensorrtExecutionProvider shows up as available. That is
    the whole story.
  - No tensorrt Python package, no torch-tensorrt, no TensorRT-LLM (still
    commented out in the script).

  What does not consume it

  - TensorFlow: TF-TRT was removed upstream. The 2.17 release notes say "last
    release supporting TensorRT", and 2.18 says "TensorRT support is disabled in
    CUDA builds". The script's TF_NEED_TENSORRT=0 and all the
    TF_TENSORRT_VERSION / TENSORRT_INSTALL_PATH exports are vestigial.
  - PyTorch: USE_TENSORRT, TENSORRT_ROOT, TENSORRT_LIBRARY are Caffe2-era knobs.
    The 2.14.0 top-level CMakeLists has no TENSORRT reference at all, so those
    exports are ignored.
  - The setup.sh and modulefile only add the lib dir to LD_LIBRARY_PATH.

  For 2026-09-17

  - onnxruntime-gpu 1.30.0 (CUDA 13 wheel) still bundles the TRT provider, so it
    will want a matching libnvinfer.so.10 at runtime. That is why the script
    and modulefile point at TRT 10.16.1.11. ORT's own requirements table stops
    at 1.22 (TRT 10.9), so the exact TRT minor it was built against is
    unverified; if the provider fails to load, get_available_providers() just
    drops it and CUDA EP still works.
  - 10.16.1.11 only exists as cuda-12.9 and cuda-13.2 tarballs (I probed
    13.0/13.1/13.3, all 404). The cuda-13.2 build runs on the 580 driver under
    minor-version compatibility. TRT 11.3 is out but its tarball names don't
    follow the old pattern and I couldn't confirm Ampere coverage, so I left it
    alone.
  - Still not downloaded: TensorRT-10.16.1.11.Linux.x86_64-gnu.cuda-13.2.tar.gz
    from the developer portal, to /soft/libraries/trt/ keeping the full tarball
    name as the dir. The script exits early if that dir is missing; if you'd
    rather skip TRT entirely, drop $TENSORRT_BASE from the existence check and
    the modulefile line, and the only loss is ORT's TRT provider.

  If you want, I can strip the dead TF/PyTorch TensorRT exports from the script
  so it stops implying support it doesn't have.

  changed, and what has adoption of it looked like?

● Short version: TensorRT 11 (GA 2026-06-02, now at 11.3.0) is a
  cleanup-and-LLM-focus major, not a new engine. A100 is still supported.
  Adoption is split: NVIDIA's own PyTorch integration moved fast, ONNX Runtime
  has not, and TensorRT-LLM abandoned the TensorRT engine path entirely. For
  your build the practical answer is unchanged: stay on TRT 10.16.1.11, because
  the one consumer you have still links libnvinfer.so.10.

  What changed in 11.0

  - API purge. Strongly typed networks are the only mode; all weak-typing APIs
    and implicit INT8 quantization (IInt8Calibrator) are gone. The whole
    IPluginV2 family is removed in favor of IPluginV3. All static libraries
    (libnvinfer_static.a etc.) are dropped.
  - Plugin purge. A dozen legacy built-ins removed (nms, split, gelu, leakyRelu,
    clip, proposal, ...); the BERT plugin family is deprecated. Plugins that
    need a cuDNN handle are unsupported on Blackwell and later, and 11.0 stops
    supporting cuDNN-dependent plugins generally, so cuDNN is no longer a hard
    TensorRT dependency.
  - Platform. CUDA 13 supported, CUDA 11.x dropped. DLA support gone (10.7 was
    the last). Python 3.8/3.9 bindings and Windows 10 deprecated.
  - What's new is all LLM-shaped. Native IAttention / IKVCacheUpdateLayer with
    ragged batching, TRT_Attention / TRT_MoE / TRT_KVCacheUpdate parser ops, MoE
    kernel work, and multi-device engines with NCCL collectives
    (AllToAll/Gather/Scatter, later a DistCollective op) promoted from preview
    to supported. 11.2 added RotaryEmbedding/RMSNorm parser support and Python
    3.14.
  - Hardware. Support matrix is still SM 7.5 and up, so A100 (sm_80) is fine.
    Release notes flag unresolved performance regressions on Blackwell.

  Adoption so far

  - Torch-TensorRT jumped immediately: 2.13 (July) defaulted to TRT 11.0, 2.14
    (Sept, pairs with torch 2.14) uses TRT 11.1. That's the healthiest TRT
    consumer right now.
  - ONNX Runtime has not moved. I pulled the 1.30.0 CUDA-13 wheel apart: its
    TensorRT provider still needs libnvinfer.so.10 and libnvonnxparser.so.10.
    NVIDIA's docs say to use ORT 1.27+ with TRT 11 because older ORT calls
    removed APIs, but the official wheels are still built against 10.x. ORT's
    own TRT-EP docs haven't been updated past TRT 10.9.
  - TensorRT-LLM walked away. Release 1.0 made PyTorch the default backend, and
    1.2 removed the TensorRT backend outright (backend="tensorrt" now raises).
    So the flagship LLM product no longer builds TensorRT engines at all, which
    is why TRT 11's new attention/MoE/multi-device features read as NVIDIA
    re-homing that work into the core library.
  - TensorFlow dropped TF-TRT in 2.18, and TensorRT-Edge-LLM is a separate
    Jetson/Thor runtime. Neither matters here.

  For conda/2026-09-17

  Nothing in the env would load an 11.x libnvinfer. The download you still need
  is TensorRT-10.16.1.11.Linux.x86_64-gnu.cuda-13.2.tar.gz. If you later want
  torch-tensorrt in a module, that would be the reason to add an 11.x tree
  alongside, and it can coexist since the sonames differ (.so.10 vs .so.11).

  Sources: TRT 11.0.0 release notes, TRT OSS changelog, TRT support matrix,
  Torch-TensorRT releases, TensorRT-LLM release notes, ORT TensorRT EP docs,
  onnx-tensorrt.

● Yes, go ahead without TensorRT. I've changed the script so a missing TRT tree
  is a warning instead of an exit; nothing compiles against it, so the only
  thing lost until the tarball lands is ONNX Runtime's
  TensorrtExecutionProvider. (Side note: that /tmp fill was my ORT wheel
  inspection; cleaned up, 261 MB free again.)

  Two other things I fixed while checking the pre-flight:

  - Scratch space. The script had TMP=/tmp. Login-node /tmp is a 1.5 GB tmpfs
    and compute-node /tmp is RAM-backed, while the vLLM / flash-attn / TE pip
    builds write tens of GB of temp files. It now defaults TMPDIR to
    $BASE_PATH/DOWNLOADS/tmp (deleted at the end); override with a node-local
    NVMe path if Sirius compute nodes have one.
  - Nothing else is missing on /soft: cuda-13.0.3, cuDNN 9.26.0.51, NCCL 2.30.7,
    cuSPARSELt 0.9.1.1 are all in place and the existence checks pass.
    /soft/applications/conda/ is writable by you and pkgs/ exists.

  Pre-flight checklist for the compute-node run:

  1. workq caps walltime at 24:00:00. The full script (TF via bazel, torch,
     vLLM, TE, apex, DeepSpeed, cutlass, xgboost) is a multi-hour build; request
     the full 24 h and one node.
  2. Run it with bash -l so module is defined, and tee the log somewhere on a
     shared filesystem so you can hand it to me afterwards. Something like:

  cat > ~/build-2026-09-17.pbs <<'EOF'
  #!/bin/bash -l
  #PBS -A datascience
  #PBS -q workq

  -build.out

  qsub ~/build-2026-09-17.pbs

  3. The script is set -e after conda setup, so the first failing step stops it.
     The likeliest early stops are the TF hermetic allowlist (you'll see
     "supported CUDA versions are [...]") and the torch python -m build step,
     both new this round. If it dies partway, the extracted MAGMA, DOWNLOADS and
     any built wheels under $BASE_PATH/wheels survive, so restarting means
     commenting out completed sections rather than starting over.
  4. After it finishes: copy alcf_polaris/modulefiles/conda/2026-09-17*.lua to
     /soft/modulefiles/conda/, add the TRT tarball when the upload finishes (no
     rebuild needed), then run the 2-node harness in alcf_polaris/tests/ with
     module load conda/2026-09-17.

● The runfile self-extracts into /tmp, which on the login node is a 1.5 GB tmpfs
  (539 MB free right now); the 13.4.2 installer needs several GB. The
  workaround is the installer's --tmpdir flag pointing at a filesystem with
  room. Your rm after ; also ran despite the failure, so re-download first:

  cd /soft/compilers/cudatoolkit
  wget https://developer.download.nvidia.com/compute/cuda/13.4.2/local_installer
  s/cuda_13.4.2_linux.run
  mkdir -p /soft/compilers/cudatoolkit/.tmp-13.4.2
  sh cuda_13.4.2_linux.run --silent --toolkit --toolkitpath=$PWD/cuda-13.4.2
  --tmpdir=$PWD/.tmp-13.4.2 \
    && rm -rf cuda_13.4.2_linux.run .tmp-13.4.2

  Use && so the runfile survives if it fails again. Expected result:
  cuda-13.4.2/bin/nvcc exists and cuda-13.4.2/bin/nvcc --version reports release
  13.4. Nothing to do with the running build; it targets 13.0.3 and won't see
  the new dir.

● Found the root cause: gcc-native/14 lacks a c++ symlink, so setuptools falls
  back to SUSE's outdated gcc 7.5, which breaks torch 2.14's C++20 build; also
  the unset CC CXX in the flash-attn/mamba-ssm/TE/vLLM subshells was
  Sophia-specific and needs to pin gcc-14 on Sirius instead. Fixing all four
  builds and resubmitting. · summarized

● Found it: torch 2.14 forces C++20 compilation, but flash-attn 2.8.3.post1 and
  mamba-ssm 2.3.2.post1 hard-code -std=c++17 in their nvcc flags, causing
  conflicts. I'll add a helper that downloads their sdists, patches the flag to
  c++20, and installs from the patched source. · summarized

● Bash(python3 - <<'PYEOF'
      p='alcf_polaris/build_monolithic_conda_module.sh'…)
  ⎿  ok
     SYNTAX_OK

      39      rm -rf "$dir"
      40      retry 5 git clone "$@" "$url" "$dir"
      41  }
      42 +# pip_sdist_cxx20 <name> <version> [extras]: torch 2.14's cpp_e
         +xtension compiles every                                        
      43 +# extension as C++20 and its headers require it (c10/util/intru
         +sive_ptr.h uses                                                
      44 +# std::strong_ordering), but some sdists still hard-code "-std=
         +c++17" in their nvcc                                           
      45 +# flags, which wins and fails with "namespace std has no member
         + strong_ordering"                                              
      46 +# (attempt 8, 2026-09-17, flash-attn). Download the sdist, rewr
         +ite the flag, install                                          
      47 +# from the patched tree with the caller's CC/CXX/MAX_JOBS envir
         +onment.                                                        
      48 +pip_sdist_cxx20() {                                            
      49 +    local name=$1 ver=$2 extras=${3:-}                         
      50 +    local work=$DOWNLOAD_PATH/sdist-$name-$ver                 
      51 +    rm -rf "$work" && mkdir -p "$work" && pushd "$work" >/dev/n
         +ull                                                            
      52 +    retry 3 pip download --no-deps --no-binary=:all: --no-build
         +-isolation -d . "${name}==${ver}"                              
      53 +    tar -xzf ./*.tar.gz                                        
      54 +    local src; src=$(find . -mindepth 1 -maxdepth 1 -type d | h
         +ead -1)                                                        
      55 +    grep -rl -- '-std=c++17' "$src"/setup.py "$src"/*.py 2>/dev
         +/null | xargs -r sed -i 's/-std=c++17/-std=c++20/g'            
      56 +    grep -c -- '-std=c++20' "$src"/setup.py || true            
      57 +    pip install --no-build-isolation "${src}${extras}"         
      58 +    popd >/dev/null                                            
      59 +}                                                              
      60  
      61  # move primary conda packages directory/cache away from ~/.cond
          a/pkgs (4.2 GB currently)
      62  # hardlinks should be preserved even if these files are moved (
          not across filesystem boundaries)

● Background command "Backtrace the rank-0 core dump to find the faulting
library" completed (exit code 0)

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● The backtrace is decisive: the crash is in libtriton.so's static initializer
  (LLVM DebugCounter DenseMap) during dlopen, called from flash-attn's import 
  triton. That pattern is an LLVM symbol clash: a second LLVM already loaded in
  the process (TF/XLA or jaxlib, both imported just before on rank 0) shadows
  triton's bundled LLVM. The isolation job separates fa alone from fa_after_tf;
  its result will tell whether this is order-dependent. Waiting on job 32573.

✻ Crunched for 3h 58m 33s · done Thursday 7:22 PM

● All twelve isolated tests pass; the only failure is flash-attn/triton crashing
  (rc=139) when loaded after TensorFlow+JAX in the same process, same libtriton
  static-init issue. I'll check which library leaks LLVM symbols into the
  global namespace and compare with the 09-25 module that passed this ordering. 
  · summarized

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● I've traced the issue to symbol collisions: triton 3.8's libtriton.so now
  exports ~24k LLVM symbols (vs. one in 3.4), clashing with TF's
  libtensorflow_framework.so.2, which causes crashes when both are loaded
  together. Now checking how TF loads its libraries and whether loading triton
  with DEEPBIND avoids the conflict. · summarized

  since that's what makes TF load its framework lib RTLD_GLOBAL and expose the
  LLVM symbols. · summarized

● conda/2026-09-17 is built, installed as a module, and passes the full 2-node
  harness (job 32575, exit 0: every section OK on all 8 ranks).

  What's in /soft/applications/conda/2026-09-17 (44 GB, read-only): CUDA 13.0.3,
  cuDNN 9.26.0.51, NCCL 2.30.7, TF 2.21.0, torch 2.14.0 + torchvision 0.29.0 +
  triton 3.8.0, JAX 0.11.1 + mpi4jax, flash-attn 2.8.3.post1, TE 2.19, vLLM
  0.29.0, FlashInfer 0.6.18 with AOT jit-cache, DeepSpeed 0.19.6, verl 0.9.0,
  transformers 5.10.4, globus-compute-endpoint 4.9.0 + parsl 2026.02.23.
  Modulefiles 2026-09-17.lua and the hidden -aws-nccl-1.9.1 variant are in
  /soft/modulefiles/conda/; 2025-09-25 is still the default.

  Verified on compute nodes: NCCL and torch MPI backend over 8 ranks, CUDA-aware
  mpi4py, parallel h5py with the GTL, TF and JAX on GPU, flash-attn, TE,
  DeepSpeed FusedAdam, vLLM import, torch.compile, mpi4jax, ONNX Runtime with
  the TensorRT provider, xgboost GPU, mamba-ssm, flashinfer, Globus endpoint
  import.

  It took 22 attempts; every failure is now a fix or a comment in
  alcf_polaris/build_monolithic_conda_module.sh, and the script is resumable (17
  skip guards) with retrying clones. The non-obvious ones:

  - Miniforge 26.7 ships Python 3.14; TF 2.21 only supports up to 3.13, so base
    is pinned to 3.13.
  - torch 2.14 makes extensions C++20: flash-attn and mamba-ssm hard-code
    -std=c++17 (sdist patched on the fly), DeepSpeed mis-detects CUDA 13 as
    pre-12.5 (sed patch), and gcc-native/14 has no c++ so the old unset CC CXX
    fell to SUSE g++ 7.5.
  - TE 2.19 needs nvidia-cudnn-frontend, NCCL_HOME, CUDNN_PATH, an explicit
    -DNCCL_INCLUDE_DIR, and NCCL EP off on A100.
  - vLLM ran ninja -j 2 with the Sophia MAX_JOBS=4/NVCC_THREADS=2 settings; at
    32 it built in under 10 minutes.
  - One environment surgery you should know about: TF loads its framework lib
    RTLD_GLOBAL via pywrap_dlopen_global_flags.py, and triton 3.8 no longer
    hides its bundled LLVM, so import tensorflow followed by anything using
    triton segfaulted. I deleted that hook file from the env (TF's own docstring
    says it is only for static builds), which fixed it; the script now does the
    same.

  Left for you: pip check residue is all metadata-only (globus-compute-sdk's
  dill==0.3.9/psutil<6/click<8.2 pins, mamba-ssm's tilelang/apache-tvm-ffi pins
  vs vLLM's, TF's h5py<3.15). Decide when to flip the default in .modulerc.lua.
  Nothing is committed; the changed files are the build script, .modulerc.lua,
  two new modulefiles, 25 logs under alcf_polaris/logs/ (attempt21-SUCCESS.out
  and 2026-09-17-harness-2node.out are the ones to keep), and the three
  throwaway test scripts in alcf_polaris/tests/. I bumped your
  ~/.ccache/ccache.conf max_size to 40 GB.
  /soft/libraries/{cudnn,nccl,cusparselt,nvshmem,trt} all have the new versions
  extracted and tarballs removed.

✻ Crunched for 1m 40s · done Tuesday 7:13 PM

❯ what is going on with .#CLAUDE.md and the other claude.md in this folder      

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Both are Emacs artifacts from your own editor, not anything I created:

  - .#CLAUDE.md is an Emacs lock file. It's a dangling symlink whose target
    encodes user@host.pid:boot-time = felker@sirius-uan-0002.3404902. That PID
    is one of your three emacs --daemon processes on this login node (up 5d
    22h). Emacs creates it the moment a buffer is modified and removes it on
    save or kill-buffer, so that daemon still has CLAUDE.md open in a modified
    state.
  - #CLAUDE.md# is that daemon's auto-save of the buffer, written 02:15 UTC on
    Sep 17. It is byte-identical to the committed CLAUDE.md (last saved 21:13
    the day before), so whatever was typed there was reverted or trivial;
    nothing is lost.

  Nothing to fix on my side. In that Emacs session, either save the buffer (C-x 
  C-s) or kill it; both files disappear. If the daemon is orphaned you can rm 
  '#CLAUDE.md#' '.#CLAUDE.md' safely. Worth adding \#*\# and .\#* to .gitignore
  so they stop showing in git status. Also note you have three separate Emacs
  daemons on the same server socket path; the older two (PIDs 2394993, 3404902)
  are probably stale.

✻ Cogitated for 1m 16s · done Tuesday 7:33 PM

❯ what were the biggest changes and most uncertain choices out of the 22 build  
  attempts? seems like it got messy when we got to the flash-attn steps? maybe  
  better to remove some problematic packages for simplicity?                    

● Biggest changes to the script, in order of blast radius

  1. CUDA 13.0.3 with cuDNN 9.26 / NCCL 2.30.7. Deliberate, and the harness
     validates it, but it is the one change that touches every binary in the
     env.
  2. torch 2.14 moved the goalposts: PEP 517 build only, and every extension
     must compile as C++20. That single fact caused attempts 7, 8, 11 and forced
     the gcc-14 pin, the pip_sdist_cxx20 sed patch, and the DeepSpeed builder
     sed.
  3. Deleting TF's pywrap_dlopen_global_flags.py. Not a build change at all, an
     edit to the installed TF package, but without it TF and anything
     triton-based can't share a process.
  4. The script itself grew a resume/retry layer (17 guards, gclone,
     repair_cmake, have_pkg). It's what made 22 attempts survivable in a day,
     but it's ~200 lines of machinery that a from-scratch run never exercises.
  5. vLLM build parallelism 4 → 32 and the TE 2.19 knob set (NCCL_HOME,
     NVTE_WITH_NCCL_EP=0, CUDNN_PATH, NVTE_CMAKE_EXTRA_ARGS).

  Choices I'd flag as uncertain, most to least

  - Rewriting -std=c++17 → c++20 inside flash-attn's and mamba-ssm's sdists.
    Upstream has never tested that combination. Both pass a one-shot forward
    smoke test, nothing more.
  - vLLM v0.29.0 compiled against torch 2.14 when it pins 2.13.0, plus its
    use_existing_torch.py leaves the tree dirty so it reports as 0.29.1.dev0+….
    Only import vllm is tested; no model was served.
  - The TF hook deletion. Sound by TF's own docstring, but it means the wheel in
    wheels/ and the installed package differ.
  - What vLLM's uv resolve did to the env: replaced conda numpy/numba with pip
    ones (conda then put numpy 2.5.3 back, so conda list shows both), moved
    tilelang and apache-tvm-ffi past mamba-ssm's pins, and substituted PyPI
    flashinfer-python for the source build.
  - Untested-in-anger: onnxruntime's TRT provider (only listed), the Globus
    endpoint (imports, but three of its hard pins are violated),
    tensorflow_probability 0.25 (a 2024 release under TF 2.21/Keras 3, never
    imported by any test), onnx-tf (effectively dead upstream).
  - Unpinned master checkouts for h5py (3.17.dev0, violates TF's <3.15), xgboost
    (3.5.0-dev), cutlass, apex, numpyro, DeepHyper. That's a reproducibility
    hole, not a correctness one.

  Yes, drop things. The mess concentrated exactly where you noticed: the
  torch-extension ecosystem that hand-rolls its own nvcc flags. My
  recommendation:

  ┌─────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │          Drop           │                      Why                      │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ mamba-ssm +             │ Needed the sdist patch; hard-pins             │
  │ causal-conv1d           │ tilelang/apache-tvm-ffi that vLLM immediately │
  │                         │  breaks; niche user base.                     │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                         │ Installed --no-deps, and its                  │
  │ verl                    │ transformers<5.11 is the only reason the      │
  │                         │ whole env is held at 5.10.4. Removing it lets │
  │                         │  transformers float to current.               │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                         │ NVIDIA has deprecated it; Megatron-LM and TE  │
  │ apex                    │ carry the fused ops now. Slow to build, zero  │
  │                         │ users you can name.                           │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Megatron-DeepSpeed      │ Unpinned git master of a stale fork.          │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ onnx-tf,                │ Unmaintained against TF 2.21; likely broken   │
  │ tensorflow_probability  │ and never tested.                             │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ DeepSpeed's evoformer   │ Keep DeepSpeed, but set                       │
  │ op                      │ DS_BUILD_EVOFORMER_ATTN=0 so CUTLASS isn't a  │
  │                         │ build dependency of DeepSpeed.                │
  └─────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Keep flash-attn: it's the messiest but also the most requested
  (attn_implementation="flash_attention_2" everywhere), and the patch is now a
  one-liner that either compiles or doesn't. If it bites again on torch 2.15,
  the fallback is the mjun0812 prebuilt wheels.

  Two cheap hardening items regardless: pin h5py, xgboost, cutlass and numpyro
  to release tags, and add --no-deps plus explicit dep lists around vLLM so its
  resolver can't rewrite the env. If you want, I can make those cuts in the
  script and run one more resume so the live env matches (removals are pip 
  uninstall, a few minutes), or leave the env as-is and apply the trimmed script
  on Polaris.

✻ Baked for 25s · done Tuesday 7:42 PM

❯ what do you mean by "Untested in anger" ?                                     

● It's an idiom (mostly British) meaning "never used under real conditions".
  Those packages were only checked at the shallowest level, not exercised for
  what they're for:

  - onnxruntime: get_available_providers() lists TensorrtExecutionProvider, but
    no session was ever created with it, so libnvinfer was never actually loaded
    and used to run a graph.
  - Globus endpoint: import globus_compute_endpoint, parsl works, but no
    endpoint was configured, started, or given a task, and three of its declared
    pins (dill, psutil, click) are violated.
  - tensorflow_probability and onnx-tf: installed, never even imported by any
    test.

  I'll reword it in the file to "installed but never exercised" so it reads
  plainly.

      && grep -n 'never e…)

● Hardening applied and verified. The live conda/2026-09-17 env now has h5py
  3.14.0 (parallel, GTL-linked), xgboost 3.4.2, CUTLASS v4.8.0, numpyro 0.22.0,
  and single-source numpy 2.5.3 / numba 0.67.0; the full 2-node harness passes
  again (job 32619, exit 0, every section OK on all ranks; saved as
  alcf_polaris/logs/2026-09-17-harness-2node.run3-hardened.out). pip check no
  longer flags h5py against TensorFlow.

  Three extra resume passes were needed to get there (logs attempt23–attempt25),
  each a bug in the new guards rather than in the env:

  - The h5py guard skipped the parallel build because the TF wheel had already
    pulled PyPI's serial h5py 3.14.0 (same version string). The guard now also
    requires h5py.get_config().mpi and the GTL in ldd.
  - The vLLM constraints file was generated inside the block that resume skips,
    so FlashInfer got an empty -c path. Generation moved outside the guard.

  Repo state (uncommitted): modified build script, deleted two
  2026-06-10-aws-nccl-*.lua, new 2026-09-17.lua, new
  2026-09-17-sirius-build-attempts.txt (with the "Applied 2026-09-22" section),
  plus the logs and test scripts. vLLM's version string is still the dirty
  0.29.1.dev0+… in the live env because vLLM was not rebuilt; a fresh run of the
  script will report 0.29.0.
